$ BOSONIC TIME.leansetadd_2share_1more_vertsettingsSourcesChatStudioarrow_back MAIN Unified Action: SAT/Blockwave Formulationbutton_magic Source guide arrow_drop_down% SAT-Blockwave_Unified_Action_Corrected.tex
\documentclass[12pt]{article}
\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb,amsfonts,physics,siunitx,geometry}
\usepackage{bm}
\geometry{margin=1in}
\setlength{\parskip}{1em}
\setlength{\parindent}{0pt}

\title{SAT / Blockwave: Unified Action — Corrected, Dimensionally Consistent Formulation}
\author{(Confidential Draft)}
\date{\today}

\begin{document}
\maketitle

\begin{abstract}
A single, dimensionally consistent formulation of the SAT/Blockwave unified-action model. This source consolidates the full field content, specifies SI-consistent units for all quantities, presents the corrected Lagrangian density, and lists the resulting coupled partial differential equations. An appendix provides line-by-line dimensional bookkeeping.
\end{abstract}

\section*{Conventions and SI base units}
We use SI base symbols for dimensional analysis: $M$ (mass), $L$ (length), $T$ (time). The metric signature is not required for this non-relativistic morphogenic presentation.

\begin{table}[h!]
\centering
\begin{tabular}{p{0.22\textwidth} p{0.58\textwidth}}
\hline
{Symbol} & {Physical meaning and SI dimensions} \\
\hline
$\rho$ & mass (energy) density, $[\rho]=M L^{-3}$ \\
$\mathbf{J}$ & mass flux density, $[\mathbf{J}]=M L^{-2} T^{-1}$ \\
$\mathbf{v}$ & velocity field, $[\mathbf{v}]=L T^{-1}$ \\
$\Phi$ & morphogenic potential per unit mass, $[\Phi]=L^{2} T^{-2}$ \\
$W(\Phi)$ & potential per unit mass (same units as $\Phi$), $[W]=L^{2} T^{-2}$ \\
$U(\Phi)=\rho W(\Phi)$ & energy density, $[U]=M L^{-1} T^{-2}$ \\
$\kappa$ & curvature coupling, $[\kappa]=M L^{-3} T^{2}$ \\
$c$ & characteristic wave speed, $[c]=L T^{-1}$ \\
$\gamma$ & damping, $[\gamma]=T^{-1}$ \\
$\lambda$ & dimensionless coupling (appears in Lagrangian as $\lambda\,\rho\,\Phi\,\nabla\cdot\mathbf v$) \\
$P$ & pressure, $[P]=M L^{-1} T^{-2}$ \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\caption{Primary symbols and their SI-base dimensions used in the text.}
\end{table}

\section{Field definitions and identities}
We define the basic fields used throughout:
\begin{align}
\mathbf v(\mathbf r,t) &= \frac{\mathbf J(\mathbf r,t)}{\rho(\mathbf r,t)}, \qquad [\mathbf v]=L T^{-1},\\
S[\Phi] &\equiv (\nabla \Phi), \qquad [\nabla \Phi]=L T^{-2} .
\end{align}

The curvature tensor for the scalar field is the rank-2 morphotensor $\mathbf C = \nabla\otimes\nabla\Phi$.

\section{Corrected Lagrangian density}
We choose a dimensionally consistent Lagrangian density (energy density units):
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lagrangian}
\mathcal L = \frac{1}{2}\rho \mathbf v^2 - \rho\,W(\Phi) - \frac{\kappa}{2}(\nabla\Phi)^2 + \lambda\,\rho\,\Phi\, (\nabla\cdot\mathbf v).
\end{equation}
Here $W(\Phi)$ is the potential per unit mass; the coupling $\lambda$ is taken dimensionless and $\rho$ supplies appropriate units in the interaction term.

\section{Euler--Lagrange system (resulting PDEs)}
Variation of $\mathcal L$ with respect to the hydrodynamic variables and $\Phi$ yields the following coupled PDE system.

\subsection*{Mass continuity}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:continuity}
\partial_t\rho + \nabla\cdot(\rho\mathbf v) = 0.
\end{equation}

\subsection*{Momentum balance}
The momentum equation (obtained from $\delta\mathcal L/\delta\mathbf v$) reads:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:momentum}
\rho\left(\partial_t\mathbf v + (\mathbf v\cdot\nabla)\mathbf v\right)
= -\nabla P - \rho\nabla W(\Phi) - \kappa\,(\nabla^2\Phi)\,\nabla\Phi + \lambda\,\nabla(\rho\Phi),
\end{equation}
where the curvature-derived body force has the form $\mathbf f_\kappa=-\kappa(\nabla^2\Phi)\,\nabla\Phi$ (see Appendix for derivation). The last term arises from the interaction piece and is dimensionally consistent when $\lambda$ is dimensionless.

\subsection*{Scalar (per-unit-mass) evolution}
The scalar evolution equation (variation w.r.t. $\Phi$) in mass-normalized form is:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:scalar}
\partial_t^2\Phi + \gamma\,\partial_t\Phi = c^2\,\nabla^2\Phi - \frac{dW}{d\Phi} + \frac{\lambda}{\rho}\,\nabla\cdot(\rho\mathbf v).
\end{equation}
Here $dW/d\Phi$ carries units of $T^{-2}$ so that each term has the same dimensions $[\Phi]/T^2 = L^2T^{-4}$.

\section{Energy and conserved quantity}
Define total energy (for $\gamma=0$) as
\begin{equation}\label{eq:energy}
E_{\text{tot}} = \int_V \left[ \tfrac{1}{2}\rho \mathbf v^2 + \rho W(\Phi) + \tfrac{\kappa}{2}(\nabla\Phi)^2 \right] dV.
\end{equation}
Direct computation, using the PDEs above, shows that $dE_{\text{tot}}/dt = 0$ when $\gamma=0$ and appropriate boundary conditions apply (see Appendix).

\section*{Dimensionless rescaling}
Introduce characteristic scales $L_0$, $T_0$, $\rho_0$, and $\Phi_0$. Define nondimensional variables
\[
\tilde{\mathbf r}=\frac{\mathbf r}{L_0},\quad \tilde t=\frac{t}{T_0},\quad \tilde\rho=\frac{\rho}{\rho_0},\quad \tilde\Phi=\frac{\Phi}{\Phi_0},\quad \tilde{\mathbf v}=\frac{\mathbf v T_0}{L_0}.
\]
The governing equations can be written in terms of dimensionless control numbers such as
\[ \mathrm{Re}_\Phi = \frac{\rho_0 L_0^2}{\kappa T_0^2},\qquad \mathrm{Ma}_\Phi = \frac{V_0}{c},\qquad \mathrm{Da}_\Phi = \frac{W(\Phi_0) T_0^2}{\Phi_0}.\]

\section*{Appendix: Dimensional bookkeeping and derivations}
\subsection*{A. Units for the curvature force}
The curvature energy density is $\tfrac{\kappa}{2}(\nabla\Phi)^2$. Variation with respect to $\Phi$ yields a functional derivative that produces a body-force density of the form
\[
\mathbf f_\kappa = -\kappa\, (\nabla^2\Phi)\,\nabla\Phi + \text{(higher-order gradient terms)}.
\]
Check units: $[\nabla^2\Phi]=T^{-2}$, $[\nabla\Phi]=L T^{-2}$, $[\kappa]=M L^{-3}T^{2}$ so $[\mathbf f_\kappa]=M L^{-2}T^{-2}$ as required for a force density.

\subsection*{B. Euler--Lagrange to PDE mapping}
A short derivation of the scalar equation: compute $\delta\mathcal L/\delta\Phi$ and apply continuity where necessary; one finds the mass-normalized wave-like equation shown in Eq.~\eqref{eq:scalar}.

\subsection*{C. Energy conservation sketch}
Multiply Eq.~\eqref{eq:momentum} by $\mathbf v$ and integrate, combine with Eq.~\eqref{eq:scalar} multiplied by $\rho\,\partial_t\Phi$, and integrate by parts. Under vanishing-flux boundary conditions the total energy time derivative vanishes for $\gamma=0$.

\end{document}

$ BOSONIC TIME.leansetadd_2share_1more_vertsettingsSourcesChatStudioarrow_backEuler-Lagrange Derivations for Corrected SAT/Blockwave Lagrangianbutton_magic Source guide arrow_drop_down% Euler-Lagrange_Derivations.tex
% Plain LaTeX source: Euler--Lagrange derivations for the corrected SAT/Blockwave Lagrangian.
\documentclass[12pt]{article}
\usepackage{amsmath,amsfonts,amssymb,physics}
\usepackage{geometry}
\geometry{margin=1in}
\setlength{\parskip}{0.8em}
\setlength{\parindent}{0pt}

\begin{document}

\begin{center}
\Large\textbf{Euler--Lagrange derivations\\
for the corrected SAT / Blockwave Lagrangian}
\end{center}

\vspace{1em}

We start from the corrected, dimensionally consistent Lagrangian density (Eq.~\eqref{L} below) used in the main text:
\begin{equation}\label{L}
\mathcal{L}(\rho,\mathbf v,\Phi)
= \tfrac{1}{2}\rho \mathbf v^2
- \rho\,W(\Phi)
- \tfrac{\kappa}{2}(\nabla\Phi)^2
+ \lambda\,\rho\,\Phi\,(\nabla\!\cdot\!\mathbf v),
\end{equation}
where \(W(\Phi)\) is the potential per unit mass (units of \(\Phi\)), \(\kappa\) is the curvature coupling, and \(\lambda\) is dimensionless. The independent fields for the variational principle are taken to be
\(\rho(\mathbf x,t),\,\mathbf v(\mathbf x,t),\,\Phi(\mathbf x,t)\).
Mass conservation (the continuity equation) is imposed as a constraint using a Lagrange multiplier \(\chi(\mathbf x,t)\). The constrained action is
\begin{equation}\label{S}
S[\rho,\mathbf v,\Phi,\chi]
= \int_{t_0}^{t_1}\!\!\int_{\Omega}
\Big\{ \mathcal L(\rho,\mathbf v,\Phi)
+ \chi\big(\partial_t\rho + \nabla\!\cdot(\rho\mathbf v)\big)
\Big\}\,d^3x\,dt .
\end{equation}

Below we compute the stationary conditions \(\delta S=0\) under arbitrary compactly-supported variations
\(\delta\rho,\;\delta\mathbf v,\;\delta\Phi,\;\delta\chi\).
We integrate by parts and drop boundary terms (assuming either vanishing variations on the boundary or appropriate boundary conditions).

\medskip
\textbf{1. Variation with respect to the Lagrange multiplier \(\chi\).}

Varying \(S\) with respect to \(\chi\) enforces the continuity constraint:
\[
\delta_{\chi}S = \int \delta\chi\;(\partial_t\rho + \nabla\!\cdot(\rho\mathbf v))\,d^3x\,dt
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\boxed{ \; \partial_t\rho + \nabla\!\cdot(\rho\mathbf v) = 0 \; . \;}
\]

\medskip
\textbf{2. Variation with respect to the velocity field \(\mathbf v\).}

Collect \(\mathbf v\)-dependent pieces of the integrand:
\[
\mathcal L \supset \tfrac{1}{2}\rho \mathbf v^2
+ \lambda\,\rho\,\Phi\,(\nabla\!\cdot\!\mathbf v)
+ \chi\,\nabla\!\cdot(\rho\mathbf v).
\]
Take the variation \(\mathbf v \mapsto \mathbf v + \delta\mathbf v\). Compute term-by-term.

(i) Kinetic term:
\[
\delta\!\bigl(\tfrac12\rho \mathbf v^2\bigr)
= \rho\,\mathbf v\cdot\delta\mathbf v \qquad (\rho \text{ treated as independent}).
\]

(ii) Interaction term:
\[
\delta\bigl(\lambda\,\rho\,\Phi\,\nabla\!\cdot\!\mathbf v\bigr)
= \lambda\,\rho\,\Phi\,\nabla\!\cdot(\delta\mathbf v)
= -\lambda\,\nabla(\rho\Phi)\cdot\delta\mathbf v
\;+\;\text{(boundary term)} .
\]
(We integrated by parts once and discarded the boundary term.)

(iii) Constraint term:
\[
\delta\bigl(\chi\,\nabla\!\cdot(\rho\mathbf v)\bigr)
= \chi\,\nabla\!\cdot(\rho\,\delta\mathbf v)
= -\rho\,\nabla\chi\cdot\delta\mathbf v
\;+\;\text{(boundary term)}.
\]

Summing (i)--(iii) and requiring stationarity for arbitrary \(\delta\mathbf v\) gives the Euler--Lagrange condition:
\[
\boxed{ \;\rho\,\mathbf v - \lambda\,\nabla(\rho\Phi) - \rho\,\nabla\chi \;=\; \mathbf 0 \;. \;}
\]
Equivalently,
\begin{equation}\label{vel_eq}
\boxed{ \;\mathbf v = \nabla\chi + \frac{\lambda}{\rho}\nabla(\rho\Phi)\;. \;}
\end{equation}
This equation expresses the velocity field in terms of the multiplier \(\chi\) (a Bernoulli-type potential) and the interaction-gradient term. It is the canonical momentum relation coming from the variational principle.

\medskip
\textbf{3. Variation with respect to the density \(\rho\).}

Collect \(\rho\)-dependent terms from the integrand:
\[
\mathcal L \supset \tfrac12\rho\mathbf v^2 - \rho W(\Phi) + \lambda\,\rho\,\Phi\,\nabla\!\cdot\!\mathbf v
+ \chi(\partial_t\rho + \nabla\!\cdot(\rho\mathbf v)).
\]
Vary \(\rho\mapsto\rho+\delta\rho\). Compute contributions (we treat \(\mathbf v,\Phi,\chi\) fixed while varying \(\rho\)).

(i) From kinetic term:
\[
\delta\!\bigl(\tfrac12\rho\mathbf v^2\bigr) = \tfrac12 \mathbf v^2 \,\delta\rho.
\]

(ii) From potential term:
\[
\delta(-\rho W(\Phi)) = -W(\Phi)\,\delta\rho.
\]

(iii) From interaction:
\[
\delta\bigl(\lambda\rho\Phi\nabla\!\cdot\!\mathbf v\bigr) = \lambda\Phi(\nabla\!\cdot\!\mathbf v)\,\delta\rho.
\]

(iv) From constraint term (two pieces):
\[
\delta\bigl(\chi\,\partial_t\rho\bigr) = \chi\,\partial_t(\delta\rho)
= -(\partial_t\chi)\,\delta\rho + \text{(boundary in time)},
\]
and
\[
\delta\bigl(\chi\,\nabla\!\cdot(\rho\mathbf v)\bigr)
= \chi\,\nabla\!\cdot(\delta\rho\,\mathbf v)
= -\mathbf v\!\cdot\!\nabla\chi\,\delta\rho + \text{(spatial boundary)}.
\]

Combining all contributions and discarding total time/spatial boundary terms, stationarity with respect to \(\delta\rho\) yields
\[
\tfrac12 \mathbf v^2 - W(\Phi) + \lambda\Phi(\nabla\!\cdot\!\mathbf v) - \partial_t\chi - \mathbf v\!\cdot\!\nabla\chi = 0 .
\]
Rearrange to the more suggestive form
\begin{equation}\label{rho_eq}
\boxed{ \;\partial_t\chi + \mathbf v\!\cdot\!\nabla\chi
= \tfrac12 \mathbf v^2 - W(\Phi) + \lambda\,\Phi\,(\nabla\!\cdot\!\mathbf v)\;. \;}
\end{equation}
This is the Bernoulli (or generalized Hamilton--Jacobi) relation for the multiplier \(\chi\).

\medskip
\textbf{4. Variation with respect to the scalar field \(\Phi\).}

Collect \(\Phi\)-dependent pieces of the integrand:
\[
\mathcal L \supset -\rho\,W(\Phi) - \tfrac{\kappa}{2}(\nabla\Phi)^2
+ \lambda\,\rho\,\Phi\,(\nabla\!\cdot\!\mathbf v).
\]
Take \(\Phi\mapsto\Phi+\delta\Phi\). Compute term-by-term.

(i) Potential term:
\[
\delta\bigl(-\rho W(\Phi)\bigr) = -\rho\,\frac{dW}{d\Phi}\,\delta\Phi .
\]

(ii) Gradient energy:
\[
\delta\!\bigl(-\tfrac{\kappa}{2}(\nabla\Phi)^2\bigr)
= -\kappa\,\nabla\Phi\!\cdot\!\nabla(\delta\Phi)
= +\kappa\,\nabla^2\Phi\,\delta\Phi
\;+\;\text{(boundary term)},
\]
after integrating by parts once and discarding boundary contributions.

(iii) Interaction term:
\[
\delta\bigl(\lambda\,\rho\,\Phi\,\nabla\!\cdot\!\mathbf v\bigr)
= \lambda\,\rho\,(\nabla\!\cdot\!\mathbf v)\,\delta\Phi .
\]

Summing these and enforcing stationarity for arbitrary \(\delta\Phi\) yields the scalar Euler--Lagrange equation
\[
-\rho\,\frac{dW}{d\Phi} + \kappa\,\nabla^2\Phi + \lambda\,\rho\,(\nabla\!\cdot\!\mathbf v) = 0.
\]
Divide by \(\rho\) (assuming \(\rho>0\)) and rearrange to the mass-normalized form used in the main text:
\begin{equation}\label{phi_EL}
\boxed{ \;\kappa\,\frac{\nabla^2\Phi}{\rho}
- \frac{dW}{d\Phi} + \lambda\,(\nabla\!\cdot\!\mathbf v) = 0\;. \;}
\end{equation}
Multiplying both sides by \(\rho\) gives the compact form
\[
\boxed{ \;-\rho\,\frac{dW}{d\Phi} + \kappa\,\nabla^2\Phi + \lambda\,\rho\,\nabla\!\cdot\!\mathbf v = 0\;. \;}
\]

\vspace{0.5em}
\textbf{Remark on the scalar's temporal dynamics:} the Lagrangian density \eqref{L} contains no explicit time-derivative term for \(\Phi\), therefore the EL equation \eqref{phi_EL} is elliptic/constraint-like rather than a dynamical wave equation. If one wishes to obtain a hyperbolic (wave) evolution for \(\Phi\) with \(\partial_t^2\Phi\) present, introduce a kinetic term in \(\mathcal L\) of the form
\[
\mathcal L_{\text{kin},\Phi} = \tfrac{\mu}{2}\,(\partial_t\Phi)^2
\]
with a phenomenological coefficient \(\mu\) (with SI units chosen so \(\mu(\partial_t\Phi)^2\) is an energy density). Adding that term and redoing the \(\Phi\)-variation yields
\[
\mu\,\partial_t^2\Phi + \kappa\,\nabla^2\Phi - \rho\,\frac{dW}{d\Phi} + \lambda\,\rho\,\nabla\!\cdot\!\mathbf v = 0,
\]
which, upon dividing by \(\mu\), reproduces the mass-normalized wave-type scalar PDE used earlier (with identification \(\mu\leftrightarrow1\) up to units).

\medskip
\textbf{5. From the variational relations to the Eulerian momentum equation.}

Equations \eqref{vel_eq} and \eqref{rho_eq} are a standard Clebsch-style parametrization. To extract an explicit Eulerian momentum-balance law (the convective form written in the main text), take a material time derivative $D_t \equiv \partial_t + \mathbf v\!\cdot\!\nabla$ of \(\nabla\chi\) and combine with \eqref{vel_eq}--\eqref{rho_eq}. The steps (sketched) are:

\begin{align}
\mathbf v &= \nabla\chi + \frac{\lambda}{\rho}\nabla(\rho\Phi), \qquad\text{(from \eqref{vel_eq})}\nonumber\\
D_t\mathbf v &= D_t(\nabla\chi) + D_t\!\Big(\frac{\lambda}{\rho}\nabla(\rho\Phi)\Big).
\end{align}
Using \(\nabla(\partial_t\chi + \mathbf v\cdot\nabla\chi)\) and substituting \eqref{rho_eq} for the combination \(\partial_t\chi + \mathbf v\cdot\nabla\chi\) yields after algebra the familiar convective momentum balance:
\[
\rho\big(\partial_t\mathbf v + (\mathbf v\cdot\nabla)\mathbf v\big)
= -\rho\,\nabla W(\Phi)
- \kappa\,(\nabla^2\Phi)\,\nabla\Phi
- \nabla P
+ \lambda\,\nabla(\rho\Phi),
\]
where the pressure term \(P\) can arise from including a thermodynamic internal-energy contribution in \(\mathcal L\) (or as a Lagrange multiplier enforcing incompressibility). The curvature body force appears precisely in the form derived earlier:
\[
\mathbf f_\kappa = -\kappa\,(\nabla^2\Phi)\,\nabla\Phi .
\]

This is the momentum equation quoted in the corrected document.

\medskip
\textbf{6. Energy identity (sketch).}

Define the total energy functional (for $\gamma=0$ and with no explicit $\partial_t\Phi$ kinetic term)
\[
E_{\text{tot}}[t] \;=\; \int_\Omega \Big( \tfrac12\rho\mathbf v^2 + \rho W(\Phi) + \tfrac{\kappa}{2}(\nabla\Phi)^2 \Big)\,d^3x.
\]
Differentiate \(E_{\text{tot}}\) with respect to time and use the continuity equation, momentum balance and the scalar EL relation to rearrange terms. After integrations by parts and assuming vanishing boundary fluxes one obtains \(dE_{\text{tot}}/dt = 0\). (If a damping term or explicit $\Phi$-kinetic dissipation is present, the right-hand side is negative definite and energy decays accordingly.)

\bigskip
\textbf{Summary of final EL system (compact):}
\begin{align*}
&\text{Continuity:}\qquad \partial_t\rho + \nabla\!\cdot(\rho\mathbf v) = 0,\\[4pt]
&\text{Momentum (convective form):}\qquad
\rho\big(\partial_t\mathbf v + (\mathbf v\!\cdot\!\nabla)\mathbf v\big)
= -\nabla P - \rho\,\nabla W(\Phi) - \kappa\,(\nabla^2\Phi)\,\nabla\Phi + \lambda\,\nabla(\rho\Phi),\\[4pt]
&\text{Scalar EL:}\qquad -\rho\,\frac{dW}{d\Phi} + \kappa\,\nabla^2\Phi + \lambda\,\rho\,\nabla\!\cdot\!\mathbf v = 0.
\end{align*}

\bigskip
\noindent\textbf{Remark.} The derivation above is fully variational and dimensionally consistent. If instead you prefer the scalar field \(\Phi\) to be explicitly dynamical (wave equation with second time derivative), add a suitable kinetic term \(\tfrac{\mu}{2}(\partial_t\Phi)^2\) to \(\mathcal L\). The same variational protocol (with the continuity multiplier) then yields the hyperbolic scalar PDE displayed in the main draft (with \(\mu\) controlling the inertial scale of \(\Phi\)).

\end{document}

Instead of treating particles and forces as fundamental fields on a pre-existing spacetime, the theory posits that reality consists of a four-dimensional tangled network of particle worldlines called filaments that intersect an advancing wavefront known as the time surface. $ BOSONIC TIME.leansetadd_2share_1more_vertsettingsSourcesChatStudioarrow_backFormalizing SAT/Blockwave Field Theorybutton_magic Source guide arrow_drop_downFormalizing SAT/Blockwave Field TheoryThis response formalizes the core mathematical structure of the SAT/Blockwave theory presented in your sources. I will define the fields, the fundamental actions, the conserved quantities, and the resulting equations of motion (EOMs) using syntax inspired by Lean/Mathlib conventions, suitable for input into a formal verification environment like lean_dojo.Since your sources contain three interconnected but distinct formalisms (3+1 Non-Relativistic Variational Dynamics, General 4D Covariant Framework, and Topological Mass Model), the code is divided into corresponding sections.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
-- ========================================================================
-- SAT/Blockwave Unified Action Model (3+1 Non-Relativistic Formalism)
-- Based on: "Euler-Lagrange Derivations for Corrected SAT/Blockwave Lagrangian"
-- Fields: ρ, v, Φ, χ defined on R^3 x R (spacetime)
-- ========================================================================

section NonRelativistic_SAT_Blockwave

variables (T L M : Type) [real_tensors T] [field_structure L] [mass_density M]
variables (x : L) (t : T)

-- Constants and coupling coefficients (Dimensionally consistent)
parameter (kappa : M * L⁻³ * T² : ℝ)  -- Curvature coupling [1]
parameter (lambda : ℝ)                 -- Dimensionless coupling [1]
parameter (W : ℝ → ℝ)                  -- Potential per unit mass [1]

-- Independent fields
def rho (x t) : ℝ := sorry             -- Mass density [1]
def v (x t) : vector L := sorry        -- Velocity field [1]
def Phi (x t) : ℝ := sorry             -- Morphogenic potential [1]
def chi (x t) : ℝ := sorry             -- Lagrange multiplier (potential) [2]

-- The Lagrangian Density (units of Energy Density) [3, 4]
def L_density (rho v Phi) : ℝ :=
  (1/2) * rho * (v.norm_sq)                  -- Kinetic energy
  - rho * (W Phi)                           -- Potential energy
  - (kappa/2) * (grad Phi).norm_sq           -- Curvature energy
  + lambda * rho * Phi * (div v)            -- Interaction term

-- The Constrained Action S[rho, v, Phi, chi] [2]
def S_constrained : ℝ :=
  integral (L_density rho v Phi + chi * (dt rho + div (rho * v))) d³x dt

-- ========================================================================
-- I. STATIONARY CONDITIONS (Euler-Lagrange Equations)
-- ========================================================================

-- Theorem: The stationary conditions (EL equations) derived from S_constrained are:

-- 1. Variation w.r.t. χ (Lagrange Multiplier)
theorem EL_chi_Continuity :
  (dt rho + div (rho * v) = 0) :=
sorry -- [5, 6]

-- 2. Variation w.r.t. v (Canonical Momentum Relation)
theorem EL_v_Canonical_Momentum :
  (v = grad chi + (lambda / rho) * (grad (rho * Phi))) :=
sorry -- [7]

-- 3. Variation w.r.t. ρ (Generalized Hamilton-Jacobi/Bernoulli Relation)
theorem EL_rho_Bernoulli :
  (dt chi + v • (grad chi) = (1/2) * (v.norm_sq) - W Phi + lambda * Phi * (div v)) :=
sorry -- [8]

-- 4. Variation w.r.t. Φ (Scalar Field Constraint/Elliptic Equation)
theorem EL_Phi_Scalar_Constraint :
  (-rho * (dW/dPhi) + kappa * (laplace Phi) + lambda * rho * (div v) = 0) :=
sorry -- [6, 9]


-- II. DERIVED EULERIAN MOMENTUM EQUATION (Convective Form)
-- Derived by combining EL_v and EL_rho via the material derivative D_t [10, 11]

parameter (P : ℝ) -- Pressure term (arising from internal energy/incompressibility) [12]

theorem Eulerian_Momentum_Balance :
  (rho * (dt v + (v • grad) v) =
    - (grad P)                                          -- Pressure gradient
    - rho * (grad (W Phi))                              -- Potential gradient
    - kappa * (laplace Phi) * (grad Phi)                -- Curvature force (f_kappa) [12]
    + lambda * (grad (rho * Phi))) :=                   -- Interaction term gradient
sorry -- [6, 11]


-- III. CONSERVATION LAW (Energy Identity)
-- Total Energy E_tot [12, 13]
def E_total (rho v Phi) : ℝ :=
  integral ( (1/2) * rho * (v.norm_sq) + rho * (W Phi) + (kappa/2) * (grad Phi).norm_sq ) d³x

-- Conservation statement (for damping γ=0 and suitable boundary conditions) [12, 14]
parameter (gamma : T⁻¹ := 0)
theorem Energy_Conservation_EOM_on_shell :
  (if gamma = 0 then dt (E_total rho v Phi) = 0 else sorry) :=
sorry

end NonRelativistic_SAT_Blockwave


-- ========================================================================
-- SAT/Blockwave 4D Covariant Framework (SAT_O 2.0)
-- Based on: "SAT-native Covariant Field Equations"
-- ========================================================================

section Covariant_SAT_O_2_0

variables (M : Type) [manilfold_4D M]

-- Fundamental geometric fields
def E_tetrad : (M → tensor_type 1 1) := sorry -- Filament bundle frame field E^I_μ [15]
def g_metric : (M → tensor_type 0 2) :=
  eta_Minkowski • E_tetrad • E_tetrad := sorry -- Emergent spacetime metric g_μν [16]
def u_flow : (M → vector_4D) := sorry         -- Unit time-flow vector u^μ [16]

-- Emergent physics fields
def Psi : (M → spinor) := sorry               -- Fermionic spinor field [17]
def A_gauge : (M → 1_form) := sorry           -- Gauge connection 1-forms A^A_μ [17]
def Q_top : (M → ℝ) := sorry                  -- Topological invariant Q(x) [17]

-- The Unified SAT Action S_SAT (Manifestly covariant) [18]
def S_SAT : ℝ :=
  integral sqrt_neg_g * (
    (c⁴ / (16*pi*G_ind)) * R_scalar_curvature g_metric  -- Einstein-Hilbert term [19]
    + L_foliation u_flow                               -- Foliation/Strain L_u [20]
    + L_gauge A_gauge                                  -- Gauge L_gauge [21]
    + L_dirac Psi                                      -- Dirac L_Dirac [22]
    + L_int (...)                                      -- Interaction L_int [23]
  ) d⁴x + S_Topological

-- ========================================================================
-- IV. COVARIANT EULER-LAGRANGE EQUATIONS
-- ========================================================================

-- 5. Emergent Einstein Equations (Metric/Tetrad Variation) [24, 25]
theorem EL_E_Emergent_Einstein (G_mu_nu T_total_mu_nu : tensor_type 0 2) :
  ( (c⁴ / (8*pi*G_ind)) * G_mu_nu g_metric = T_total_mu_nu ) :=
sorry -- T_total is the sum of T^(u), T^(gauge), T^(Dirac), etc.

-- 6. Dirac Equation (Fermion Variation) [26]
parameter (T_tension : M * T⁻² : ℝ) -- Filament Tension [22]
parameter (ell_f : L : ℝ)          -- Filament length [22]
def m_Psi_emergent (Q : ℝ) : M :=
  (T_tension * ell_f) / (c^2 * Q) [22]

theorem EL_Psi_Dirac_Equation :
  ( (i * hbar * gamma_mu * D_mu - m_Psi_emergent Q_top * c) * Psi = 0 ) :=
sorry -- [26]

-- 7. Yang-Mills/Maxwell Equations (Gauge Variation) [26]
def g_eff_sq_inv (rho_G : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  B_factor * rho_G * ell_f^2 -- Effective coupling dependent on linking density [27]

theorem EL_A_Yang_Mills :
  ( covariant_nabla_mu (g_eff_sq_inv rho_G * F_mu_nu_A) + Gauge_Self_Interaction = J_matter_nu_A ) :=
sorry -- [26, 28]

end Covariant_SAT_O_2_0

-- ========================================================================
-- TOPOLOGICAL MASS HIERARCHY
-- Based on: "SAT/Blockwave Topological Mass Hierarchy Derivation"
-- ========================================================================

section Mass_Hierarchy_Model

-- Bare Mass Scale [29]
def m_bare (T_tension ell_f c) : M :=
  (T_tension * ell_f) / (c^2) [29]

-- Topological Invariant Q (Dimensionless integer classification) [30]
def Q_topological (L_link W_writhe n_wind) : ℝ :=
  L_link + W_writhe + n_wind -- Simplified definition [30]

-- Topological Mass Suppression [30]
def m_topological (m_0 Q) : M :=
  m_0 / Q [30]

-- Induced Inertial Mass (from coupling to timesheet fluctuations) [31]
parameter (C g₁ M_sq E_overlap : ℝ)
def m_induced : M :=
  C * (g₁^2 / M_sq) * E_overlap [31]

-- Total Effective Mass Formula [32]
def m_effective : M :=
  m_topological m_bare Q_topological + m_induced [32, 33]

-- Quadratic Mass Spectrum (for excitation modes N) [34]
parameter (k beta a : M²)
def m_spectrum (Q N) : M :=
  sqrt (k * (N + beta * Q^2 - a)) [33, 34]

-- Dominance Condition (Topological Mass dominates induced inertia) [32]
axiom Topological_Dominance :
  (m_induced / m_topological) ≪ 1 [32]

end Mass_Hierarchy_Model

-- ========================================================================
-- NOETHER'S THEOREM (General Field Theory Statement)
-- Based on: "Noether's Theorem: Derivation for Mechanics and Fields"
-- ========================================================================

section Noether_Theorem

variables (L : Type) [field_theory_L L]
variables (phi_a : L → ℝ) (x_mu : vector 4)

-- Assume action invariance under infinitesimal transformation [35, 36]
parameter (Xi_mu : vector 4)     -- Coordinate shift generator
parameter (Delta_phi_a : ℝ)      -- Field variation generator
parameter (F_mu : vector 4)      -- Total divergence of L change [35]

-- Define the corrected field variation at fixed x: [35]
def Delta_hat_phi_a (Xi_mu Delta_phi_a) : ℝ :=
  Delta_phi_a - Xi_mu • (partial_mu phi_a)

-- Noether's Current J^μ [37]
def J_mu_Noether : vector 4 :=
  (partial_L / partial_partial_mu_phi_a) • (Delta_hat_phi_a)
  + L_density * Xi_mu
  - F_mu

-- Theorem: Conservation of the Noether Current (On-Shell) [37]
theorem Noether_Current_Conservation :
  (if EOM_satisfied phi_a then partial_mu (J_mu_Noether) = 0 else sorry) :=
sorry

-- Definition of the Conserved Charge Q [38]
def Q_Noether : ℝ :=
  integral (J_mu_Noether.component 0) d³x

theorem Charge_Conservation_Over_Time :
  (if EOM_satisfied phi_a then dt Q_Noether = 0 else sorry) :=
sorry

end Noether_Theorem

$ BOSONIC TIME.leansetadd_2share_1more_vertsettingsSourcesChatStudioarrow_backGeometric Particle Mass and Emergent Constantsbutton_magic Source guide arrow_drop_downMANUSCRIPT PAGES.

A Parameter-Free Geometric Model for Particle Masses: Worldline Filaments, the Time Wavefront, and Emergent Constants
Abstract (Condensed and Quantitative)
The Scalar–Angular–Torsion (SAT) theory proposes that reality is a four-dimensional tangled network of particle worldlines called filaments. We present a covariant geometric formalism with four fundamental fields in which these filaments interact with an advancing time wavefront (Σ 
t
​
). The core of the model is built upon a single, dimensionally consistent action. A single physical anchor fixes the absolute scale, adhering strictly to the Normalization Principle (Zero-Parameter Economy); all dimensionless constants and particle masses follow uniquely from the geometry. We derive the unified Euler–Lagrange system, demonstrate conserved quantities, and produce quantitative predictions for the Standard Model mass spectrum based on topological charge (Q) and chiral torsion (τ 
χ
​
). The topological mass prediction mechanism achieves a residual error of 0.55% in the light hadron sector, outperforming next-to-next-to-leading order chiral perturbation theory (χPT). We identify novel constraints, such as the UV Finiteness Lock (Q≤3 stable bundles), and discuss testable signatures like the 4.5% gauge coupling unification at μ

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. Postulates and Fundamental Geometric Fields
The SAT framework rests on a minimal axiomatic basis defined by four emergent fields, where all observable physics stems from the topology and statistics of the underlying 1D filament set (F). The theory adheres to the Normalization Principle, requiring that all dimensional input reduces to only two constants: filament tension (T) and fundamental length (ℓ 
f
​
).
1.1 Core Fields (SAT-Native)
The dynamics are governed by four fundamental fields whose algebra replaces conventional physics structures:
1. Time Field (T(x)) and Unit Time-Flow Vector (u 
μ
): The gradient of the scalar time field u 
μ
=∂ 
μ
T defines the Unit Time-Flow Vector, which is orthogonal to the advancing time surface (Σ

2. Geometric Emergene Scalar (Φ / Misalignment Angle θ

μ
).
3. Compact Phase Field (ψ): A U(1)-valued field encoding internal phase and winding structure. Its holonomy quantization mechanism is predicted to yield intrinsic Spin and Flavor.
4. Topological Twist Field (τ): A discrete Z 
3
​
-valued field that enforces topological conservation laws and is the origin of SU(3) color confinement.

2.2 Structural Integrity and Stability
The rigorous coherence of the theory depends on ensuring that the dynamics of the Unit Time-Flow Vector (u

1. Dirac–Bergmann Analysis: The classical stability of the emergent temporon mode (δu

4. Mass Generation Mechanism and Spectrum
Particle mass is not intrinsic but is an emergent property encoded in the geometry and topology of the filament bundles.

5. Geometric Unification and Gauge Sectors
The Standard Model gauge group SU(3)×SU(2)×U(1) is derived structurally from the topology and phase structure of the filament network.
• SU(3) Color: Derived from the dynamical Z

• Unification Precision: Evaluating the two-loop RG evolution shows that the gauge couplings unify to within 4.5% accuracy at the high unification scale (μ

6. Falsifiability and Structural Constraints
The predictive capability of SAT is anchored by several structural constraints and explicit, testable predictions.

       $ BOSONIC TIME.leanset  14 sources·May 11, 2025The provided sources introduce the Scalar-Angular-Torsion (SAT) framework, an innovative theoretical model that attempts to unify General Relativity, Quantum Mechanics, and the Standard Model through a shared geometric foundation. Instead of treating particles and forces as fundamental fields on a pre-existing spacetime, the theory posits that reality consists of a four-dimensional tangled network of particle worldlines called filaments that intersect an advancing wavefront known as the time surface. Within this unified geometry, fundamental properties such as mass, spin, and gauge couplings emerge naturally from topological structures, bundle linking numbers, and intersection angles without relying on arbitrary numerical tuning. However, critical analyses and critiques highlight significant mathematical and conceptual hurdles within the framework, pointing out inconsistencies such as ungrounded pressure terms, mixed non-relativistic and covariant formalisms, and missing derivations for key sectors like electroweak gauge fields and gravitational metric variations. Despite these formal challenges, the framework successfully generates testable predictions—such as novel ground-state particle masses, Regge slopes, and cosmological parameters—demonstrating how complex physical phenomena might arise from minimal topological and geometric primitives.keep_pinSave to notekeep_pincopythumb_upthumb_downHow does Scalar-Angular Torsion theory unify spacetime and quantum mechanics?subdirectory_arrow_rightWhat role do topological filaments play in deriving particle masses and gauge couplings?subdirectory_arrow_rightHow do critics evaluate the mathematical consistency and predictive claims of the SAT framework?subdirectory_arrow_rightkeyboard_arrow_down docs  (14) arrow_upwardGemini Notebook can make mistakes, so double-check it.🎉 New: You can now create Interactive Reports! API Reference lean_dojo.constants Edit on GitHub
lean_dojo.constants
Constants controlling LeanDojo’s behaviors. Many of them are configurable via Environment Variables.

lean_dojo.constants.CACHE_DIR= PosixPath('/home/docs/.cache/lean_dojo')
Cache directory for storing traced repos (see Caching).

lean_dojo.constants.DISABLE_REMOTE_CACHE= False
Whether to disable remote caching (see Caching) and build all repos locally.

lean_dojo.constants.LEAN4_PACKAGES_DIR= PosixPath('.lake/packages')
The directory where Lean 4 dependencies are stored (since v4.3.0-rc2).

lean_dojo.constants.LEAN4_URL= 'https://github.com/leanprover/lean4'
The URL of the Lean 4 repo.

lean_dojo.constants.LOAD_USED_PACKAGES_ONLY= False
Only load depdendency files that are actually used by the target repo.

lean_dojo.constants.NUM_PROCS= 2
Number of processes to use

lean_dojo.constants.REMOTE_CACHE_URL= 'https://dl.fbaipublicfiles.com/lean-dojo'
URL of the remote cache (see Caching).

lean_dojo.constants.TACTIC_CPU_LIMIT= 1
Number of CPUs for executing tactics when interacting with Lean.

lean_dojo.constants.TACTIC_MEMORY_LIMIT= '32g'
Maximum memory when interacting with Lean.

lean_dojo.constants.TMP_DIR= None
Temporary directory used by LeanDojo for storing intermediate files

lean_dojo.constants.check_git_version(min_version: Tuple[int, int, int])→ None[source]
Check the version of Git installed on the system.

API Reference lean_dojo.constants Edit on GitHub
lean_dojo.constants
Constants controlling LeanDojo’s behaviors. Many of them are configurable via Environment Variables.

lean_dojo.constants.CACHE_DIR= PosixPath('/home/docs/.cache/lean_dojo')
Cache directory for storing traced repos (see Caching).

lean_dojo.constants.DISABLE_REMOTE_CACHE= False
Whether to disable remote caching (see Caching) and build all repos locally.

lean_dojo.constants.LEAN4_PACKAGES_DIR= PosixPath('.lake/packages')
The directory where Lean 4 dependencies are stored (since v4.3.0-rc2).

lean_dojo.constants.LEAN4_URL= 'https://github.com/leanprover/lean4'
The URL of the Lean 4 repo.

lean_dojo.constants.LOAD_USED_PACKAGES_ONLY= False
Only load depdendency files that are actually used by the target repo.

lean_dojo.constants.NUM_PROCS= 2
Number of processes to use

lean_dojo.constants.REMOTE_CACHE_URL= 'https://dl.fbaipublicfiles.com/lean-dojo'
URL of the remote cache (see Caching).

lean_dojo.constants.TACTIC_CPU_LIMIT= 1
Number of CPUs for executing tactics when interacting with Lean.

lean_dojo.constants.TACTIC_MEMORY_LIMIT= '32g'
Maximum memory when interacting with Lean.

lean_dojo.constants.TMP_DIR= None
Temporary directory used by LeanDojo for storing intermediate files

lean_dojo.constants.check_git_version(min_version: Tuple[int, int, int])→ None[source]
Check the version of Git installed on the system.

API Reference lean_dojo.constants Edit on GitHub
lean_dojo.constants
Constants controlling LeanDojo’s behaviors. Many of them are configurable via Environment Variables.

lean_dojo.constants.CACHE_DIR= PosixPath('/home/docs/.cache/lean_dojo')
Cache directory for storing traced repos (see Caching).

lean_dojo.constants.DISABLE_REMOTE_CACHE= False
Whether to disable remote caching (see Caching) and build all repos locally.

lean_dojo.constants.LEAN4_PACKAGES_DIR= PosixPath('.lake/packages')
The directory where Lean 4 dependencies are stored (since v4.3.0-rc2).

lean_dojo.constants.LEAN4_URL= 'https://github.com/leanprover/lean4'
The URL of the Lean 4 repo.

lean_dojo.constants.LOAD_USED_PACKAGES_ONLY= False
Only load depdendency files that are actually used by the target repo.

lean_dojo.constants.NUM_PROCS= 2
Number of processes to use

lean_dojo.constants.REMOTE_CACHE_URL= 'https://dl.fbaipublicfiles.com/lean-dojo'
URL of the remote cache (see Caching).

lean_dojo.constants.TACTIC_CPU_LIMIT= 1
Number of CPUs for executing tactics when interacting with Lean.

lean_dojo.constants.TACTIC_MEMORY_LIMIT= '32g'
Maximum memory when interacting with Lean.

lean_dojo.constants.TMP_DIR= None
Temporary directory used by LeanDojo for storing intermediate files

API Reference lean_dojo.constants Edit on GitHub
lean_dojo.constants
Constants controlling LeanDojo’s behaviors. Many of them are configurable via Environment Variables.

lean_dojo.constants.CACHE_DIR= PosixPath('/home/docs/.cache/lean_dojo')
Cache directory for storing traced repos (see Caching).

lean_dojo.constants.DISABLE_REMOTE_CACHE= False
Whether to disable remote caching (see Caching) and build all repos locally.

lean_dojo.constants.LEAN4_PACKAGES_DIR= PosixPath('.lake/packages')
The directory where Lean 4 dependencies are stored (since v4.3.0-rc2).

lean_dojo.constants.LEAN4_URL= 'https://github.com/leanprover/lean4'
The URL of the Lean 4 repo.

lean_dojo.constants.LOAD_USED_PACKAGES_ONLY= False
Only load depdendency files that are actually used by the target repo.

lean_dojo.constants.NUM_PROCS= 2
Number of processes to use

lean_dojo.constants.REMOTE_CACHE_URL= 'https://dl.fbaipublicfiles.com/lean-dojo'
URL of the remote cache (see Caching).

lean_dojo.constants.TACTIC_CPU_LIMIT= 1
Number of CPUs for executing tactics when interacting with Lean.

lean_dojo.constants.TACTIC_MEMORY_LIMIT= '32g'
Maximum memory when interacting with Lean.

lean_dojo.constants.TMP_DIR= None
Temporary directory used by LeanDojo for storing intermediate files

lean_dojo.constants.check_git_version(min_version: Tuple[int, int, int])→ None[source]
Check the version of Git installed on the system.

© Copyright 2023, LeanDojo Team.

lean_dojo.data_extraction.lean
This module define classes for repos, files, and theorems in Lean. Objects of these classes contain only surface information, without extracting any trace.

API Reference lean_dojo.data_extraction lean_dojo.data_extraction.trace Edit on GitHub
lean_dojo.data_extraction.trace
This module provides the main interfaces for tracing Lean repos, i.e., extracting data from them. To estimate the time for tracing a repo, a good rule of thumb is 1.5x the time for compiling the repo using leanpkg build. A repo has to be traced only once, and the traced repo will be stored in a cache for fast access in the future.

lean_dojo.data_extraction.trace.check_files(packages_path: Path, no_deps: bool)→ None[source]
Check if all *.lean files have been processed to produce *.ast.json and *.dep_paths files.

lean_dojo.data_extraction.trace.get_lean_version()→ str[source]
Get the version of Lean.

lean_dojo.data_extraction.trace.get_traced_repo_path(repo: LeanGitRepo, build_deps: bool = True)→ Path[source]
Return the path of a traced repo in the cache.

The function will trace a repo if it is not available in the cache. See Caching for details.

Parameters
:
repo (LeanGitRepo) – The Lean repo to trace.

build_deps (bool) – Whether to build the dependencies of repo. Defaults to True.

Returns
:
The path of the traced repo in the cache, e.g. /home/kaiyu/.cache/lean_dojo/leanprover-community-mathlib-2196ab363eb097c008d4497125e0dde23fb36db2

Return type
:
Path

lean_dojo.data_extraction.trace.trace(repo: LeanGitRepo, dst_dir: str | Path | None = None, build_deps: bool = True)→ TracedRepo[source]
Trace a repo (and its dependencies), saving the results to dst_dir.

The function only traces the repo when it’s not available in the cache. Otherwise, it directly copies the traced repo from the cache to dst_dir. See Caching for details.

Parameters
:
repo (LeanGitRepo) – The Lean repo to trace.

dst_dir (Union[str, Path]) – The directory for saving the traced repo. If None, the traced repo is only saved in the cahe.

build_deps (bool) – Whether to build the dependencies of repo. Defaults to True.

Returns
:
A TracedRepo object corresponding to the files at dst_dir.

Return type
:
TracedRepo

get_annotated_tactic()→ Tuple[str, List[Dict[str, Any]]][source]
Return the tactic annotated with premise information.

Premises in the tactic are marked by ... . For example, rw [add_comm b] contains a premise add_comm and therefore becomes rw [add_comm b]. In addition, the function returns the provenance (full name, file path, line/column numbers) of all premises.

Returns
:
The first return value is the tactic string marked by ... . The second return value is a list of provenances.

Return type
:
Tuple[str, List[Dict[str, Any]]]

propertystart: Pos
Start position in *.lean file.


propertystate_after: str
Pretty-printed state after applying the tactic.


propertystate_before: str
Pretty-printed state before applying the tactic.


propertytactic: str
The raw tactic string.

API Reference lean_dojo.constants Edit on GitHub
lean_dojo.constants
Constants controlling LeanDojo’s behaviors. Many of them are configurable via Environment Variables.

lean_dojo.constants.CACHE_DIR= PosixPath('/home/docs/.cache/lean_dojo')
Cache directory for storing traced repos (see Caching).

lean_dojo.constants.DISABLE_REMOTE_CACHE= False
Whether to disable remote caching (see Caching) and build all repos locally.

lean_dojo.constants.LEAN4_PACKAGES_DIR= PosixPath('.lake/packages')
The directory where Lean 4 dependencies are stored (since v4.3.0-rc2).

lean_dojo.constants.LEAN4_URL= 'https://github.com/leanprover/lean4'
The URL of the Lean 4 repo.

lean_dojo.constants.LOAD_USED_PACKAGES_ONLY= False
Only load depdendency files that are actually used by the target repo.

lean_dojo.constants.NUM_PROCS= 2
Number of processes to use

lean_dojo.constants.REMOTE_CACHE_URL= 'https://dl.fbaipublicfiles.com/lean-dojo'
URL of the remote cache (see Caching).

lean_dojo.constants.TACTIC_CPU_LIMIT= 1
Number of CPUs for executing tactics when interacting with Lean.

lean_dojo.constants.TACTIC_MEMORY_LIMIT= '32g'
Maximum memory when interacting with Lean.

lean_dojo.constants.TMP_DIR= None
Temporary directory used by LeanDojo for storing intermediate files

lean_dojo.constants.check_git_version(min_version: Tuple[int, int, int])→ None[source]
Check the version of Git installed on the system.

Limitations Edit on GitHub
Limitations
LeanDojo has the following limitations. Addressing them won’t be our priority in the near future, but we welcome contributions:

LeanDojo cannot extract data from the lean4 repo itself nor interact with theorems in it.

Currently, LeanDojo cannot process Lean repos that use FFI, e.g., [LeanCopilot](https://github.com/lean-dojo/LeanCopilot).

LeanDojo does not support term-based proofs or proofs that mix tactics and terms.

Theorems extracted by LeanDojo are “syntactic theorems”, i.e., they are Lean constants defined using keywords theorem or lemma. First, they are not guaranteed to be real theorems (Lean constants of type Prop). Second, theorems defined in other ways (e.g., using def) are not extracted.

       $ BOSONIC TIME.leanset  14 sources·May 11, 2025The provided sources introduce the Scalar-Angular-Torsion (SAT) framework, an innovative theoretical model that attempts to unify General Relativity, Quantum Mechanics, and the Standard Model through a shared geometric foundation. Within this unified geometry, fundamental properties such as mass, spin, and gauge couplings emerge naturally from topological structures, bundle linking numbers, and intersection angles without relying on arbitrary numerical tuning. However, critical analyses and critiques highlight significant mathematical and conceptual hurdles within the framework, pointing out inconsistencies such as ungrounded pressure terms, mixed non-relativistic and covariant formalisms, and missing derivations for key sectors like electroweak gauge fields and gravitational metric variations. $ BOSONIC TIME.leansetadd_2share_1more_vertsettingsSourcesChatStudioarrow_backNoether's Theorem: Derivation for Mechanics and Fieldsbutton_magic Source guide arrow_drop_down% Noether_Theorem_Derivation.tex
% Clean LaTeX source: derivation of Noether's theorem (finite-dimensional and field-theoretic forms)
\documentclass[12pt]{article}
\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb,amsfonts}
\usepackage{geometry}
\geometry{margin=1in}
\setlength{\parskip}{0.8em}
\setlength{\parindent}{0pt}

\begin{document}

\begin{center}
\LARGE\textbf{Noether's Theorem — Derivation}
\end{center}

\bigskip
\noindent
This note derives Noether's theorem in two closely related settings:

\begin{itemize}
\item[(A)] finite-dimensional Lagrangian mechanics (particle systems), and
\item[(B)] classical field theory on spacetime (the standard, general form).
\end{itemize}

The structure is: assume a continuous one-parameter family of transformations leaves the action invariant (up to a divergence). From the stationarity of the action we build a conserved current and associated conserved charge.

\section*{A. Finite-dimensional Lagrangian mechanics}

Let the configuration coordinates be \(q^i(t)\) with Lagrangian \(L(q,\dot q,t)\). The action is
\[
S[q] = \int_{t_1}^{t_2} L(q(t),\dot q(t),t)\,dt.
\]

\subsection*{Symmetry hypothesis}
Suppose there is a one-parameter family of transformations
\[
q^i(t) \mapsto q^i(t) + \varepsilon\,\Delta q^i(q,t) + \mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^2),
\qquad t \mapsto t + \varepsilon\,\Delta t(q,t) + \mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^2),
\]
under which the Lagrangian changes at most by a total time derivative:
\[
L(q+\varepsilon\Delta q,\dot q + \varepsilon \Delta\dot q, t+\varepsilon\Delta t)
= L(q,\dot q,t) + \varepsilon\,\frac{d}{dt}F(q,t) + \mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^2),
\]
for some function \(F(q,t)\). (When \(F=0\) the Lagrangian is strictly invariant.)

\subsection*{Variation of the action}
Compute the first-order change in \(S\) under the infinitesimal transformation. Let \(\delta q^i = \varepsilon\Delta q^i\) and \(\delta t = \varepsilon\Delta t\). The induced variation of the action (keeping fixed integration endpoints in the original parameter \(t\)) is
\[
\delta S = \int_{t_1}^{t_2} \Big( \frac{\partial L}{\partial q^i}\delta q^i
+ \frac{\partial L}{\partial \dot q^i}\delta\dot q^i + \frac{\partial L}{\partial t}\delta t \Big)\,dt.
\]
Integrate the $\delta\dot q^i$ term by parts:
\[
\int_{t_1}^{t_2} \frac{\partial L}{\partial \dot q^i}\delta\dot q^i\,dt
= \left.\frac{\partial L}{\partial \dot q^i}\delta q^i\right|_{t_1}^{t_2}
- \int_{t_1}^{t_2} \frac{d}{dt}\!\Big(\frac{\partial L}{\partial \dot q^i}\Big)\delta q^i\,dt.
\]
Collecting terms and using the Euler–Lagrange equations
\(\displaystyle \frac{d}{dt}\!\Big(\frac{\partial L}{\partial \dot q^i}\Big) - \frac{\partial L}{\partial q^i} = 0\),
the bulk integral vanishes for variations that follow the equations of motion. The remaining boundary contribution plus explicit total-derivative change of the Lagrangian (the \(F\)-term) yields
\[
\delta S = \left.\frac{\partial L}{\partial \dot q^i}\delta q^i\right|_{t_1}^{t_2}
+ \int_{t_1}^{t_2} \frac{d}{dt}\bigl(F - L\,\delta t\bigr)\,dt.
\]
Thus, for an on-shell trajectory (satisfying EOM) and when the action is invariant (i.e. $\delta S=0$ for arbitrary fixed-endpoint symmetry variations), we get
\[
\left.\left(\frac{\partial L}{\partial \dot q^i}\Delta q^i - F + L\,\Delta t\right)\right|_{t_1}^{t_2} = 0.
\]
Because the times \(t_1,t_2\) are arbitrary, the quantity in parentheses is constant in time. Define the Noether charge (conserved quantity)
\[
Q \;=\; \frac{\partial L}{\partial \dot q^i}\Delta q^i - F + L\,\Delta t.
\]
Then \(dQ/dt = 0\) along solutions of the Euler--Lagrange equations. This is Noether's theorem in mechanics.

\section*{B. Field-theoretic Noether theorem (classical fields)}

Let \(\phi^a(x)\) be a finite collection of fields on spacetime (coordinates \(x^\mu\), \(\mu=0,\dots,d-1\)), with Lagrangian density \(\mathcal{L}(\phi,\partial_\mu\phi,x)\). The action is
\[
S[\phi] = \int_\Omega \mathcal{L}(\phi,\partial\phi,x)\,d^dx.
\]

\subsection*{Infinitesimal transformation and Lagrangian variation}
Consider an infinitesimal continuous transformation parameterized by \(\varepsilon\):
\[
x^\mu \mapsto x'^\mu = x^\mu + \varepsilon\,\Xi^\mu(x) + \mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^2),\qquad
\phi^a(x) \mapsto \phi'^a(x') = \phi^a(x) + \varepsilon\,\Delta\phi^a(x) + \mathcal{O}(\varepsilon^2).
\]
Equivalently we can describe the field variation at the same spacetime point,
\[
\delta\phi^a(x) \equiv \phi'^a(x) - \phi^a(x) = \varepsilon\,\hat\Delta\phi^a(x)
\]
where \(\hat\Delta\phi^a(x) = \Delta\phi^a(x) - \Xi^\mu(x)\partial_\mu\phi^a(x)\) (this subtracts the effect of the coordinate change).

Assume the Lagrangian density changes by at most a total divergence:
\[
\delta\mathcal{L} \equiv \mathcal{L}'(x) - \mathcal{L}(x)
= \varepsilon\,\partial_\mu F^\mu(x)
\]
for some local \(F^\mu(x)\). This is the standard invariance condition: action is invariant (up to boundary terms).

\subsection*{Variation of the action and current construction}
Compute the first-order change in the action under the transformation. The variation of the action (for compact domain or decaying fields so boundary terms vanish after including divergence) is
\[
\delta S
= \int_\Omega \biggl( \frac{\partial\mathcal L}{\partial\phi^a}\delta\phi^a
+ \frac{\partial\mathcal L}{\partial(\partial_\mu\phi^a)}\delta(\partial_\mu\phi^a)
+ \partial_\mu(\mathcal L\,\Xi^\mu) \biggr)\,d^dx,
\]
where the \(\partial_\mu(\mathcal L\,\Xi^\mu)\) term accounts for the change in the integration measure due to the coordinate shift \(x\mapsto x+\varepsilon\Xi\). Expand \(\delta(\partial_\mu\phi^a) = \partial_\mu(\delta\phi^a)\) and integrate the derivative term by parts:
\[
\int_\Omega \frac{\partial\mathcal L}{\partial(\partial_\mu\phi^a)}\partial_\mu(\delta\phi^a)\,d^dx
= -\int_\Omega \partial_\mu\!\Big(\frac{\partial\mathcal L}{\partial(\partial_\mu\phi^a)}\Big)\delta\phi^a\,d^dx
+ \text{(boundary)}.
\]
Collect the bulk terms:
\[
\delta S = \int_\Omega \Bigg\{ \Big(\frac{\partial\mathcal L}{\partial\phi^a} - \partial_\mu\!\frac{\partial\mathcal L}{\partial(\partial_\mu\phi^a)}\Big)\delta\phi^a
+ \partial_\mu\Big[ \frac{\partial\mathcal L}{\partial(\partial_\mu\phi^a)}\delta\phi^a + \mathcal L\,\Xi^\mu \Big] \Bigg\} d^dx.
\]
If the fields satisfy the Euler--Lagrange equations,
\[
\frac{\partial\mathcal L}{\partial\phi^a} - \partial_\mu\!\frac{\partial\mathcal L}{\partial(\partial_\mu\phi^a)} = 0,
\]
then only the total divergence term survives on-shell. Using the invariance assumption \(\delta\mathcal L = \partial_\mu F^\mu\) we can equate (on-shell)
\[
\partial_\mu\Big[ \frac{\partial\mathcal L}{\partial(\partial_\mu\phi^a)}\delta\phi^a + \mathcal L\,\Xi^\mu \Big] = \partial_\mu F^\mu.
\]
Bring the right-hand side over to the left and define the Noether current
\[
\boxed{ \;J^\mu(x) \;=\; \frac{\partial\mathcal L}{\partial(\partial_\mu\phi^a)}\hat\Delta\phi^a
\;+\; \mathcal L\,\Xi^\mu \;-\; F^\mu \; }
\]
(notice we used $\hat\Delta\phi^a=\delta\phi^a$ the field variation at fixed $x$). Then the invariance condition implies the local conservation law (on-shell):
\[
\boxed{ \;\partial_\mu J^\mu(x) = 0\;. \;}
\]

This is Noether's theorem: every continuous symmetry (leaving the action invariant up to a divergence) yields a conserved current \(J^\mu\). The associated conserved charge is
\[
Q = \int_{\Sigma} J^0(x)\,d^{d-1}x
\]
where \(\Sigma\) is a spacelike Cauchy surface; time evolution plus \(\partial_\mu J^\mu=0\) implies \(dQ/dt=0\) (under suitable boundary conditions).

\subsection*{Comments and important special cases}
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Global internal symmetry:} if \(\Xi^\mu=0\) and \(\delta\phi^a=\varepsilon\,\Delta\phi^a\) with \(\delta\mathcal L=0\) (strict invariance), then the Noether current reduces to
\[
J^\mu = \frac{\partial\mathcal L}{\partial(\partial_\mu\phi^a)}\Delta\phi^a .
\]
Example: phase rotations of a complex scalar field give electric charge current.

\item \textbf{Spacetime translations:} take \(\Xi^\mu = \varepsilon^\mu\) (constant), \(\delta\phi^a = -\varepsilon^\nu\partial_\nu\phi^a\). With \(F^\mu=0\) one obtains the canonical energy--momentum tensor
\[
T^\mu_{\ \nu} = \frac{\partial\mathcal L}{\partial(\partial_\mu\phi^a)}\partial_\nu\phi^a - \delta^\mu_{\ \nu}\mathcal L,
\]
and conservation \(\partial_\mu T^\mu_{\ \nu}=0\) (Noether currents associated to translations). \(T^{00}\) is the energy density; \(T^{0i}\) are momentum densities.

\item \textbf{Gauge (local) symmetries:} if the parameter \(\varepsilon\) is a function of spacetime, the naive current vanishes on-shell (Noether second theorem) and instead yields identities among the Euler--Lagrange equations (gauge identities). For local gauge symmetries, conserved charges require careful handling (Gauss-law constraints).
\end{itemize}

\section*{Concise proof summary}
\begin{enumerate}
\item Start with an infinitesimal symmetry transformation of fields and coordinates that changes the Lagrangian by a total divergence.
\item Compute the variation of the action and integrate-by-parts the derivative terms.
\item Use the Euler--Lagrange equations to kill bulk terms; the remaining piece is a spacetime divergence.
\item Define the Noether current as the combination appearing inside the divergence; invariance implies its divergence vanishes on-shell.
\item Integrate $J^0$ over spatial slice to obtain a conserved charge.
\end{enumerate}

\bigskip
\noindent\textbf{References for further reading:} This derivation follows the standard presentations found in many classical mechanics and field theory texts (e.g.\ Goldstein, Srednicki, Peskin \& Schröder, Noether's original paper). For gauge theories and the subtleties of local symmetries see treatments of the Noether second theorem and constrained Hamiltonian systems (Dirac-Bergmann formalism).

\end{document}

$ BOSONIC TIME.leansetadd_2share_1more_vertsettingsSourcesChatStudioarrow_backSAT-native Covariant Field Equationsbutton_magic Source guide arrow_drop_down% SAT_O2_SAT-native_Covariant_Equations.tex
% SAT_O 2.0 — SAT-native, fully 4D covariant formulation and derived field equations.
% Compact, self-contained LaTeX source. Assumes units with c and ħ explicit.
\documentclass[11pt]{article}
\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb,amsfonts,mathrsfs,physics}
\usepackage{geometry}
\geometry{margin=1in}
\setlength{\parskip}{0.6em}
\setlength{\parindent}{0pt}

\begin{document}

\begin{center}
\Large\textbf{SAT\_O 2.0 — SAT-native Covariant Equations}\\
\large Full 4D geometric rewrite: unified variational construct, EL equations, and mapping log
\end{center}

\bigskip
This document gives a single, fully 4D-covariant presentation of the SAT/Blockwave (SAT\_O) framework. It (i) defines the emergent spacetime geometry from filamentary/twist fields, (ii) writes a single unified action built from purely geometric and topological SAT fields, (iii) lists the Euler--Lagrange equations (Einstein-like, gauge, Dirac, scalar, and foliation/strain), (iv) provides explicit identifications (mapping log) between standard physics structures and SAT constructs, and (v) flags key deviations and operational interpretations.

\vspace{1em}
\hrule
\vspace{0.6em}

\section*{A. Axioms and primitives (SAT-native)}

Work on a 4-dimensional differentiable manifold \(M\) (no prior metric). The basic SAT primitives are:

\begin{enumerate}
\item A \emph{filament bundle frame field} (local 1-form/tetrad sections) \(E^I{}_\mu(x)\), \(I=0,\dots,3\). These are fields on \(M\) that encode local filament directions and packing. They are not assumed orthonormal a priori; an internal Minkowski form \(\eta_{IJ}=\mathrm{diag}(-1,1,1,1)\) is introduced as an algebraic fiber metric to build a spacetime metric from the frame:
\[
g_{\mu\nu}(x) \;\equiv\; \eta_{IJ}\,E^I{}_\mu(x)\,E^J{}_\nu(x).
\]
The tetrad is \emph{emergent} (a functional of filament microstate), but treated here as a field variable for the variational principle.

\item A \emph{unit time-flow vector} (foliation) \(u^\mu(x)\). In SAT this is identified with the timelike frame direction:
\[
u^\mu = \frac{E^{0\mu}}{\sqrt{-g_{\alpha\beta}E^{0\alpha}E^{0\beta}}},\qquad u^\mu u_\mu = -1.
\]
(Enforced by a multiplier in the action.)

\item A compact \emph{phase field} \(\psi(x)\in S^1\) (U(1)-valued) and a misalignment angle \(\theta_4(x)\in S^1\) controlling disformal factors and phase-locking.

\item A discrete \emph{twist/topological field} \(\tau(x)\in\mathbb Z_N\) (locally encoded as integer-valued defects or as a density current of linking).

\item A \emph{linking density} \(\rho_G(x)\) and derived topological invariant \(Q(x)\) built from filament linking/writhe/winding; these appear as dimensionless scalar densities.

\item Gauge connection 1-forms \(A^A_\mu(x)\) (with \(A\) an index in the emergent Lie algebra, e.g. \(\mathfrak{u}(1),\mathfrak{su}(N)\)), and fermionic spinor fields \(\Psi(x)\) defined on the spinor bundle induced by the tetrad.
\end{enumerate}

All tensor operations (raising/lowering, covariant derivatives) use the Levi-Civita connection \(\nabla_\mu\) built from \(g_{\mu\nu}\) (which itself is built from \(E^I{}_\mu\)). Thus there is no background metric independent of filament/tetrad fields.

\vspace{0.8em}
\section*{B. The unified SAT action}

Define the single action (all fields dynamical):
\[
\boxed{%
S_{\text{SAT}}[E,u,\psi,\theta_4,\tau,A,\Psi,\Phi]
= \int_M \!\! d^4x\; \sqrt{-g}\;
\Biggl[
\frac{c^4}{16\pi G_{\text{ind}}}\,R[g]
+ \mathcal{L}_u
+ \mathcal{L}_{\theta_4}
+ \mathcal{L}_{\psi}
+ \mathcal{L}_{\text{gauge}}
+ \mathcal{L}_{\text{Dirac}}
+ \mathcal{L}_{\text{int}}
\Biggr]\;+\; S_{\text{Top}}.
}
\]
All pieces are SAT-native: \(G_{\text{ind}}\) will be derived (parametrically) from filament microparameters; \(S_{\text{Top}}\) collects discrete topological contributions (proportional to \(\hbar\)).

We now define each block in manifestly covariant form (indices raised/lowered with \(g\)).

\subsection*{B.1 Geometry / tetrad sector}
Tetrad determinant \(e=\det(E^I{}_\mu)\), metric \(g_{\mu\nu}=\eta_{IJ}E^I{}_\mu E^J{}_\nu\). The Ricci scalar built from \(g\) is \(R[g]\).

We include an explicit tetrad-regularization term (to penalize filament shear at microscale):
\[
\mathcal{L}_{\text{tetrad}} \;=\; -\frac{\zeta}{2\ell_f^2}\,g^{\mu\alpha}g^{\nu\beta}\,\bigl(\nabla_\mu E^I{}_\nu\bigr)\bigl(\nabla_\alpha E_{I\beta}\bigr),
\]
where \(\zeta\) is dimensionless and \(\ell_f\) is the microscopic filament length.

\subsection*{B.2 Foliation / strain block \(\mathcal{L}_u\)}
Define the strain (timesheet) tensor
\[
S_{\mu\nu} \;=\; \nabla_\mu u_\nu.
\]
Use the minimal derivative action:
\[
\boxed{%
\mathcal{L}_u \;=\; -\frac{\kappa}{\ell_f^2}\Big( S_{\mu\nu}S^{\mu\nu} - \lambda\, (S^\mu{}_\mu)^2 \Big)
\;+\; \Lambda_0 + \Lambda_\lambda (u^\mu u_\mu + 1).
}
\]
\(\Lambda_\lambda\) enforces \(u^\mu u_\mu=-1\). All contractions use \(g\).

\subsection*{B.3 Compact-phase and misalignment \(\mathcal{L}_{\psi},\mathcal{L}_{\theta_4}\)}
\[
\mathcal{L}_\psi \;=\; -\frac{f_\psi^2}{2c^2}\,(\nabla_\mu\psi)(\nabla^\mu\psi) - V_\psi(\psi),
\qquad
\mathcal{L}_{\theta_4} \;=\; -\frac{f_\theta^2}{2c^2}\,(\nabla_\mu\theta_4)(\nabla^\mu\theta_4) - V_\theta(\theta_4).
\]

\subsection*{B.4 Gauge block \(\mathcal{L}_{\text{gauge}}\)}
Let \(F^A_{\mu\nu} = \partial_\mu A^A_\nu - \partial_\nu A^A_\mu + f^{ABC}A^B_\mu A^C_\nu\) (covariantized with \(\nabla\) where necessary). The effective inverse coupling is a local function of linking density:
\[
g^{-2}_{\text{eff}}(x) \;=\; \mathcal{B}\,\rho_G(x)\,\ell_f^2,
\qquad
e^{-2}_{\text{eff}}(x) \;=\; \mathcal{B}'\,\rho_G(x)\,\ell_f^2 .
\]
Then
\[
\mathcal{L}_{\text{gauge}} \;=\; -\frac{1}{4}\,g^{-2}_{\text{eff}}(x)\,F^A_{\mu\nu}F^{A\,\mu\nu}
-\frac{1}{4}\,e^{-2}_{\text{eff}}(x)\,F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}.
\]

\subsection*{B.5 Dirac sector}
Spin connection \(\omega_\mu{}^{IJ}\) determined by tetrad. Covariant derivative on spinors \(D_\mu = \partial_\mu + \tfrac{1}{4}\omega_\mu{}^{IJ}\gamma_{IJ} + i g_{\text{eff}}A^A_\mu T^A + i e_{\text{eff}} A_\mu\). The Dirac Lagrangian:
\[
\mathcal{L}_{\text{Dirac}} \;=\; \bar\Psi\Big( i\hbar\,\gamma^\mu D_\mu - m_\Psi(x)c \Big)\Psi,
\]
with emergent mass
\[
\boxed{ \; m_\Psi(x) \;=\; \frac{T\,\ell_f}{c^2}\,\frac{1}{Q(x)} \; }.
\]
Here \(T\) is filament tension; \(Q(x)\) is topological invariant built from linking/writhe.

\subsection*{B.6 Topological action \(S_{\text{Top}}\)}
Topological couplings (axion-like and linking terms):
\[
S_{\text{Top}} \;=\; \hbar\!\int_M \sqrt{-g}\,\Big[ \gamma_1\,\psi\,\frac{1}{8\pi^2} F^A\!\wedge\!F^A + \gamma_2\,\tau\,\mathcal{I}_{\text{link}} \Big] .
\]
In components the first term is \(\propto \hbar\int \sqrt{-g}\,\psi\,F^A_{\mu\nu}\tilde F^{A\,\mu\nu} d^4x\).

\subsection*{B.7 Interaction block \(\mathcal{L}_{\text{int}}\)}
Contains Yukawa-like couplings, couplings between foliation strain and other fields, and topological mass operators:
\[
\mathcal{L}_{\text{int}} \;=\; -y(\psi,\theta_4,\tau)\,\bar\Psi\Psi\,\Phi_{\text{top}}(\theta_4,\tau)
+ \chi_1\,S^{\mu\nu}T^{(\text{gauge})}_{\mu\nu} + \cdots
\]
with \(y\) determined by topology; \(\Phi_{\text{top}}\) is scalar built from linking invariants.

\vspace{0.6em}
\section*{C. Euler--Lagrange equations (SAT-native covariant field equations)}

Vary \(S_{\text{SAT}}\) w.r.t. the independent fields \(E^I{}_\mu,\,u^\mu,\,\psi,\,\theta_4,\,A^A_\mu,\,\bar\Psi,\Psi\) (and Lagrange multipliers). Present major resulting equations.

\subsection*{C.1 Emergent Einstein equations (metric variation / tetrad variation)}

Varying tetrad (or equivalently metric) yields:
\[
\boxed{%
\frac{c^4}{8\pi G_{\text{ind}}}\,G_{\mu\nu}[g] \;=\; T^{(u)}_{\mu\nu} + T^{(\theta)}_{\mu\nu} + T^{(\psi)}_{\mu\nu} + T^{(\text{gauge})}_{\mu\nu} + T^{(\text{Dirac})}_{\mu\nu} + T^{(\text{int})}_{\mu\nu} + T^{(\text{tetrad})}_{\mu\nu},
}
\]
where each \(T^{(\cdot)}_{\mu\nu}\) is the stress-energy tensor derived in the usual covariant way: \(T_{\mu\nu} = -(2/\sqrt{-g})\delta(\sqrt{-g}\mathcal{L})/\delta g^{\mu\nu}\). The left-hand prefactor uses an induced \(G_{\text{ind}}\) determined by strain/tetrad microphysics (see Sec.~E).

\subsection*{C.2 Foliation/strain EOM (variation w.r.t. \(u^\mu\))}

Variation of \(\mathcal{L}_u\) (including coupling to other sectors) yields the nonlinear elastic-timeflow equation:
\[
\boxed{%
\frac{\kappa}{\ell_f^2}\Big( \nabla^\alpha S_{\alpha\mu} - \lambda\,\nabla_\mu S^\alpha{}_\alpha\Big)
+ \mathcal{C}_\mu[u,\psi,\theta_4,A,\Psi] + 2\Lambda_\lambda u_\mu = 0,
}
\]
where \(\mathcal{C}_\mu\) collects coupling currents (exchange of momentum with gauge, scalar, and Dirac sectors). Linearizing around a background \(u^\mu=\bar u^\mu + \delta u^\mu\) gives wave operators whose speeds depend on \(\kappa/\ell_f^2\) and background coefficients.

\subsection*{C.3 Scalar / phase EOMs}

\[
\boxed{%
f_\psi^2\,\Box_g \psi + \frac{\hbar}{8\pi^2}\,F^A_{\mu\nu}\tilde F^{A\,\mu\nu} + \frac{\partial V_\psi}{\partial\psi} + \mathcal{J}_\psi = 0,
}
\]
\[
\boxed{%
f_\theta^2\,\Box_g \theta_4 + \frac{\partial V_\theta}{\partial\theta_4} + \mathcal{J}_{\theta} = 0,
}
\]
where \(\Box_g \equiv g^{\mu\nu}\nabla_\mu\nabla_\nu\) and \(\mathcal{J}\) are source terms from interactions.

\subsection*{C.4 Yang--Mills / Maxwell equations (gauge variation)}

\[
\boxed{%
\nabla_\mu\!\bigl( g^{-2}_{\text{eff}}(x)\,F^{A\,\mu\nu}\bigr)
+ f^{ABC}g^{-2}_{\text{eff}}(x)\,A^B_\mu F^{C\,\mu\nu}
\;=\; J^{A\,\nu}_{\text{matter}} \;+\; \frac{\hbar\,\gamma_1}{8\pi^2}\,\nabla_\mu(\psi\,\tilde F^{A\,\mu\nu}),
}
\]
and the abelian Maxwell form
\[
\boxed{%
\nabla_\mu\!\bigl( e^{-2}_{\text{eff}}(x)\,F^{\mu\nu}\bigr) = J^\nu_{\text{EM}} + \frac{\hbar\,\gamma_1}{8\pi^2}\,\nabla_\mu(\psi\,\tilde F^{\mu\nu}).
}
\]
Note the explicit dependence of effective couplings on \(\rho_G(x)\) makes these equations spatially varying in coupling — a SAT-native hallmark.

\subsection*{C.5 Dirac equation (fermion variation)}

\[
\boxed{%
\bigl( i\hbar\,\gamma^\mu D_\mu - m_\Psi(x)c \bigr)\,\Psi = 0,
}
\]
with covariant derivative \(D_\mu\) including the spin connection and emergent gauge couplings. The mass \(m_\Psi(x) = T\ell_f/(c^2 Q(x))\) depends on topological invariant \(Q(x)\).

\subsection*{C.6 Coupled-mode kernel (linearization compact form)}

Collect small perturbations \(\Psi \equiv (\delta E,\delta u,\delta\psi,\delta\theta_4,\delta A,\delta\Psi)\). Symbolically:
\[
\boxed{ (\Box_g \,\mathbb{I} + \mathcal{K}[g,u,\psi,\theta_4,\rho_G,\dots])\,\Psi = 0, }
\]
where \(\mathcal{K}\) is a matrix differential operator encoding mode couplings (strain–metric mixing, axion–gauge mixing, Yukawa masses, etc.). The dispersion relations follow from \(\det(-\omega^2 + |\mathbf k|^2 + \widetilde{\mathcal K}(\omega,\mathbf k))=0\).

\vspace{0.6em}
\section*{D. SAT \(\to\) Standard-Model mapping log (short)}

Below are principal identifications (import log) used to interpret SAT equations operationally.

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Spacetime metric \(g_{\mu\nu}\)} \(\longleftrightarrow\) emergent bilinear of filament tetrads \(E^I{}_\mu\).
\item \textbf{Levi-Civita connection \(\nabla\)} \(\longleftrightarrow\) metric connection computed from \(g_{\mu\nu}\) (no background).
\item \textbf{Gravitational degrees of freedom} \(\longleftrightarrow\) long-wavelength modes of the strain tensor \(S_{\mu\nu}\) and slow variations in \(E^I{}_\mu\).
\item \textbf{Electromagnetic field strength \(F_{\mu\nu}\)} \(\longleftrightarrow\) antisymmetric part of twist/shear in filament congruences (projected 2-form density built from filament linking currents).
\item \textbf{Yang--Mills gauge group} \(\longleftrightarrow\) algebra of filament linking classes (e.g. \(\mathbb Z_3\) filament linking $\to$ SU(3) color via an emergent Lie algebra embedding).
\item \textbf{Charge quantization / \(e,\hbar\)} \(\longleftrightarrow\) compactness of \(\psi\), topological coupling coefficients in \(S_{\text{Top}}\).
\item \textbf{Particle mass} \(m\) \(\longleftrightarrow\) topological suppression \(m\sim T\ell_f/(c^2 Q)\) with \(Q\) the linking/writhe/winding invariant.
\item \textbf{Axion/CP terms} \(\longleftrightarrow\) \(\psi F\tilde F\) coupling arising naturally from topological coupling of compact phase \(\psi\) to gauge instanton density.
\end{itemize}

\vspace{0.4em}
\section*{E. Emergent constants (parametric relations)}

These are scaling relations (symbolic); numeric values require microphysical calculation.

\[
\boxed{ G_{\text{ind}} \;\sim\; \frac{c^4\,\ell_f^2}{16\pi\,\mathcal{A}\,\kappa}, \qquad
g^{-2}_{\text{eff}} \sim \mathcal{B}\,\rho_G\,\ell_f^2, \qquad
m \sim \frac{T\,\ell_f}{c^2\,Q}. }
\]

Here \(\mathcal A,\mathcal B\) are coarse-graining geometry factors computed from filament ensemble averages. \(T,\ell_f,\kappa,\rho_G\) are microscopic physical parameters (tension, filament length, strain stiffness, linking density).

\vspace{0.4em}
\section*{F. Operational interpretation and observables}

\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Metric observables:} geodesics, curvature invariants \(R,R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}\), gravitational waves correspond to collective strain excitations \(\delta u,\delta E\).
\item \textbf{Gauge observables:} field strengths \(F_{\mu\nu}\), flux integrals, linking densities measurable as Aharonov–Bohm phases (via \(\psi\) couplings).
\item \textbf{Particle masses / spectra:} computed from \(Q\) classification of minimal topological excitations and their local tension \(\times\) length scales.
\item \textbf{Coupling-space variability:} spatial/temporal variation in \(\rho_G(x)\) yields running and local modulation of effective gauge coupling constants.
\end{itemize}

\vspace{0.4em}
\section*{G. Deviation flags and caveats}

\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{No strict Lorentz background:} Lorentz invariance is emergent; tests of Lorentz symmetry bound the allowed anisotropy and gradient scale of \(u^\mu,\,E^I{}_\mu\).
\item \textbf{Coupling locality:} \(g^{-2}_{\text{eff}}(x)\) depending on \(\rho_G(x)\) makes gauge equations non-uniform; renormalization-group treatment must incorporate topological-statistical inputs.
\item \textbf{Spin structure:} spin-bundle existence requires tetrad to be globally orientable and admit spin structure; topologically nontrivial filament configurations may obstruct a global spin structure — physical interpretation: fermions live in regions where spin-bundle trivialization holds (defects = exotic states).
\item \textbf{Numerical prefactors:} derivations above give parametric scaling. To obtain precise \(G,\hbar,e,m\) one must compute coarse-graining integrals over the filament microstate ensemble (partition-function calculation).
\item \textbf{Stability/ghosts:} ensure positivity of kinetic terms (no ghosts) by constraining \(\kappa,f_\psi,f_\theta>0\) and verifying sign of induced Einstein term.
\end{enumerate}

\vspace{0.4em}
\section*{H. Recipe to operationalize (brief)}

\begin{enumerate}
\item Choose a filament micromodel (loop ensemble with tension \(T\), bending rigidity, linking weights).
\item Compute ensemble averages: \(\mathcal A,\mathcal B,\rho_G(x)\) and coarse-grained stress-energy contributions.
\item Insert into the SAT action; compute \(G_{\text{ind}}\), \(g_{\text{eff}}(x)\), mass spectrum \(m(Q)\).
\item Linearize around appropriate background and obtain mode spectra; compare with observed GR/SM data to bound \(\ell_f,\kappa,T\).
\end{enumerate}

\vspace{0.6em}
\hrule
\vspace{0.6em}

\noindent\textbf{Closing note.} The above is a compact, fully covariant SAT-native rewrite of gravity, gauge, scalar and fermion dynamics. It keeps strictly to 4D tensor form, uses only fields emergent from filament/tetrad/topology primitives, and presents explicit equations ready for a micro-to-macro coarse-graining program. Numerical realization requires specification of a filament statistical microphysics and computation of the coarse-graining kernels \(\mathcal A,\mathcal B,\rho_G\).

\end{document}

       $ BOSONIC TIME.leanset  14 sources·May 11, 2025The provided sources introduce the Scalar-Angular-Torsion (SAT) framework, an innovative theoretical model that attempts to unify General Relativity, Quantum Mechanics, and the Standard Model through a shared geometric foundation. Instead of treating particles and forces as fundamental fields on a pre-existing spacetime, the theory posits that reality consists of a four-dimensional tangled network of particle worldlines called filaments that intersect an advancing wavefront known as the time surface. Within this unified geometry, fundamental properties such as mass, spin, and gauge couplings emerge naturally from topological structures, bundle linking numbers, and intersection angles without relying on arbitrary numerical tuning. However, critical analyses and critiques highlight significant mathematical and conceptual hurdles within the framework, pointing out inconsistencies such as ungrounded pressure terms, mixed non-relativistic and covariant formalisms, and missing derivations for key sectors like electroweak gauge fields and gravitational metric variations.        $ BOSONIC TIME.leanset  14 sources·May 11, 2025The provided sources introduce the Scalar-Angular-Torsion (SAT) framework, an innovative theoretical model that attempts to unify General Relativity, Quantum Mechanics, and the Standard Model through a shared geometric foundation. Instead of treating particles and forces as fundamental fields on a pre-existing spacetime, the theory posits that reality consists of a four-dimensional tangled network of particle worldlines called filaments that intersect an advancing wavefront known as the time surface. Within this unified geometry, fundamental properties such as mass, spin, and gauge couplings emerge naturally from topological structures, bundle linking numbers, and intersection angles without relying on arbitrary numerical tuning. However, critical analyses and critiques highlight significant mathematical and conceptual hurdles within the framework, pointing out inconsistencies such as ungrounded pressure terms, mixed non-relativistic and covariant formalisms, and missing derivations for key sectors like electroweak gauge fields and gravitational metric variations. Despite these formal challenges, the framework successfully generates testable predictions—such as novel ground-state particle masses, Regge slopes, and cosmological parameters—demonstrating how complex physical phenomena might arise from minimal topological and geometric primitives.keep_pinSave to notekeep_pincopythumb_upthumb_downHow does Scalar-Angular Torsion theory unify spacetime and quantum mechanics?subdirectory_arrow_rightWhat role do topological filaments play in deriving particle masses and gauge couplings?subdirectory_arrow_rightHow do critics evaluate the mathematical consistency and predictive claims of the SAT framework?subdirectory_arrow_rightkeyboard_arrow_down docs  (14) arrow_upwardGemini Notebook can make mistakes, so double-check it.arrow_backdeleteBased on the sources provided, I can present the starting Lagrangian density and the final system of coupled partial differential equations (PDEs) that result from applying the Euler-Lagrange (EL) formalism.However, the sources do not contain the detailed, step-by-step mathematical derivation (the "showing your work" component) required to transition from the Lagrangian density to the final PDEs. They explicitly state that the variation of the Lagrangian yields the final system12.Below, I outline the required components for the derivation and list the final derived equations provided in the text.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. The Starting Point: The Lagrangian Density ($\mathcal L$)The derivation begins with the corrected, dimensionally consistent Lagrangian density ($\mathcal L$), which has units of energy density34:$$\mathcal L = \frac{1}{2}\rho \mathbf v^2 - \rho\,W(\Phi) - \frac{\kappa}{2}(\nabla\Phi)^2 + \lambda\,\rho\,\Phi\, (\nabla\cdot\mathbf v). \quad \text{$$3$$}$$This Lagrangian depends on the fluid density ($\rho$), the velocity field ($\mathbf v$), and the morphogenic potential field ($\Phi$)35.The Euler-Lagrange formalism generally requires taking the functional derivatives (variations) of the action integral $\mathcal{A} = \int \mathcal{L} \, d^3x \, dt$ with respect to the fundamental fields, setting the results to zero ($\delta\mathcal{A}/\delta\text{Field} = 0$).2. The Resulting Euler-Lagrange Equations (The PDEs)The variation of $\mathcal L$ with respect to the hydrodynamic variables ($\rho$ and $\mathbf v$) and the scalar field ($\Phi$) yields the following coupled PDE system1:A. Variation with respect to $\rho$ (Mass Continuity)The sources list the standard Mass continuity equation as part of the resulting system1:$$ \partial_t\rho + \nabla\cdot(\rho\mathbf v) = 0. \quad \text{$$1$$} $$B. Variation with respect to $\mathbf v$ (Momentum Balance)The source states that the Momentum balance equation is obtained specifically from the variation $\delta\mathcal L/\delta\mathbf v$1.The resulting equation is:$$ \rho\left(\partial_t\mathbf v + (\mathbf v\cdot\nabla)\mathbf v\right) = -\nabla P - \rho\nabla W(\Phi) - \kappa\,(\nabla^2\Phi)\,\nabla\Phi + \lambda\,\nabla(\rho\Phi). \quad \text{$$6$$} $$The term $\mathbf f_\kappa = -\kappa\,(\nabla^2\Phi)\,\nabla\Phi$ is the curvature-derived body force6. The sources confirm that the units for this force density are dimensionally consistent, matching $M L^{-2}T^{-2}$7. The last term, $\lambda\,\nabla(\rho\Phi)$, arises from the interaction piece and is consistent because $\lambda$ is dimensionless6.C. Variation with respect to $\Phi$ (Scalar Evolution)The source confirms that the Scalar evolution equation results from the variation with respect to $\Phi$26. The source mentions that a "short derivation" involves computing $\delta\mathcal L/\delta\Phi$ and applying continuity where necessary2.The resulting mass-normalized, wave-like equation is:$$ \partial_t^2\Phi + \gamma\,\partial_t\Phi = c^2\,\nabla^2\Phi - \frac{dW}{d\Phi} + \frac{\lambda}{\rho}\,\nabla\cdot(\rho\mathbf v). \quad \text{$$8$$} $$For this equation to be dimensionally consistent, the term $dW/d\Phi$ must carry units of $T^{-2}$, ensuring all terms have the dimensions $[\Phi]/T^2 = L^2T^{-4}$8.infoSaved responses are view onlyconvert_to_text Convert to source
